\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}=\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{${\mathrm{grad}\,}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.44444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.41145pt\hskip-2.41145pt\hbox{$q$}\hskip-2.41145pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.41145pt}
)

\begin{cases}-\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}=\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\hskip-2.98613pt\hbox{$f$}\hskip-2.98613pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.98613pt},&\quad\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\[4.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}={\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}+\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt})-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt},&\quad\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\[4.0pt]
\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}=0,&\quad\mbox{on $\Gamma_{D}$,}\\[4.0pt]
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\cdot\hbox{\hskip 3.00117pt\hskip-3.00117pt\hbox{$n$}\hskip-3.00117pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.00117pt}=\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$0$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt},&\quad\mbox{on $\Gamma_{N}$.}\end{cases}

(45)

In this form we can apply the results in
33
,
13
,
46
to arrive at
the weak (fiber) formulation:
find
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\in\Sigma:=H_{0,\Gamma_{N}}({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
and

\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}\in\mathbb{U}:=H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle b_{\mathfrak{p}}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}],[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}])

\displaystyle:=(-\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip \displaystyle=(\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\hskip-2.98613pt\hbox{$f$}\hskip-2.98613pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip (46)

We define as in the previous section the operator

{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathbb{H}:=\Sigma\times\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{L}_{2}:=L_{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times L_{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
by
({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}])=b_{\mathfrak{p}}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}])
for all
([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}],[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}])\in\mathbb{H}\times\mathbb{L}_{2}
, i.e.,

{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}))\choose-\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}}.

It has been shown in
33
,
13
,
46
that

{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
an isomorphism. Corresponding inf-sup- or continuity constants depend in general on
\mathfrak{p}
so that any uniform stability depends on the range permitted in
\mathfrak{P}
.
Similarly to the error-residual relation (
29
) for the case of the stationary diffusion problem, we obtain, for any approximation
[\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}]
of the exact solution
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]
at any given parameter
\mathfrak{p}
, the fiber-error-residual relation

\|[\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}]-[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\eqsim\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p})\quad\forall\,[\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}]\in\mathbb{H},\,\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P},

(47)

where the residual
\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p})
of the approximation
[\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}]
at parameter
\mathfrak{p}
is given by

\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}^{\circ}-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt})+{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})}^{2}+\|\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 2.98613pt}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(48)

This is for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
an easily computable loss function that enters
the corresponding mean-squared loss in (
6
) which is used to train the surrogate models.

Training yields then
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*})]\in\mathbb{X}=L_{\mu}^{2}(\mathfrak{P};\mathbb{H})

from which we recover an approximation to the solution of (
45
) by

\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{u}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}(\mathfrak{p})=\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*}))+\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt},\quad\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\hskip-2.77779pt\hbox{$\tilde{\sigma}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}$}\hskip-2.77779pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.77779pt}(\mathfrak{p})=\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})+\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}.

(49)

We immediately infer from
5
that the exact
solution
[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]\in\mathbb{X}
of (
45
) satisfies

\|\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*})]-[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]\|^{2}_{\ell_{2}(\widehat{\mathfrak{P}};\mathbb{H})}\eqsim\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\theta^{*})];\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}).

(50)

4  Conforming finite element approximations

To evaluate the residual losses (
30
) (diffusion) and (
48
) (elasticity), we employ conforming Galerkin FOSLS discretizations with mixed finite-element (FE) pairs
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
and discrete boundary lifts. We formulate the discrete problems with lifted boundary conditions and, under uniform ellipticity, establish (i) variational error–residual equivalence and (ii) convergence rates for the FE losses evaluated at the FE solutions.

4.1  Conforming finite element approximation of the diffusion problem

For the diffusion problem and each parameter
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, we compute the conforming FE approximation
[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},{\tilde{u}}^{\circ}]=[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}]\in\mathbb{H}_{h}
of the lifted fiber solution as

\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(x,\mathfrak{p})=\sum_{n=1}^{N_{h}^{\sigma}}\sigma^{\circ}_{n}(\mathfrak{p})\varphi_{n}^{\sigma}(x)\text{ and }u^{\circ}_{h}(x,\mathfrak{p})=\sum_{n=1}^{N_{h}^{u}}u_{n}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})\varphi_{n}^{u}(x),

(51)

where
\bm{\sigma}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})=(\sigma^{\circ}_{1}(\mathfrak{p}),\dots,\sigma^{\circ}_{N_{h}^{\sigma}}(\mathfrak{p}))^{\top}
and
\bm{u^{\circ}}(\mathfrak{p})=(u_{1}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),\dots,u^{\circ}_{N_{h}^{u}}(\mathfrak{p}))^{\top}
are the parametric coefficient vectors,
(\varphi_{n}^{\sigma})_{n=1}^{N_{h}^{\sigma}}
and
(\varphi_{n}^{u})_{n=1}^{N_{h}^{u}}
are the basis functions of the FE space
\mathbb{H}_{h}=\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}\subset H({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Omega)
, defined over a mesh with element size
h
. Here
N_{h}^{\sigma}
and
N_{h}^{u}
are the number of degrees of freedom (DoFs) of the FE spaces
\Sigma_{h}
and
\mathbb{U}_{h}
, respectively. Specifically, we employ the conforming FE pairs

\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}=\text{RT}_{k}^{\circ}\times\text{CG}_{m}^{\circ},\quad k\geq 0,\;m\geq 1,

where
\text{RT}_{k}
is the
Raviart–Thomas element

39
of order
k
(which has polynomial degree
\leq k+1
),
H({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega)
-conforming vector fields with continuous normal fluxes (see, e.g.,
DefElement
for details), and CG
m
is the
continuous Galerkin element
(or Lagrange element) of degree
m
, with piecewise polynomials of degree
\leq m
on each cell and
C^{0}
-continuous across cell interfaces, thus
H^{1}(\Omega)
-conforming scalar fields. Here
\text{RT}^{\circ}_{k}
and
\text{CG}^{\circ}_{m}
denote the subspaces with the essential boundary conditions incorporated, i.e.,
\tau\cdot n=0
on
\Gamma_{N}
for any
\tau\in\text{RT}^{\circ}_{k}
and
v=0
on
\Gamma_{D}
for any
v\in\text{CG}^{\circ}_{m}
, practically by setting the corresponding DoFs to zero.

For each
\mathfrak{p}
we compute the FE approximation of the solution
[\sigma^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]
by solving the Galerkin system

\displaystyle({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}],{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tau_{h},v_{h}])_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=([\mathfrak{p}\nabla w_{h}-z_{h}+f_{1},f_{2}],{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tau_{h},v_{h}])_{\Omega},\quad\forall\,[\tau_{h},v_{h}]\in\mathbb{H}_{h},

(52)

which reads in explicit terms as: find
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u_{h}^{\circ}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
such that

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma^{\circ}_{h}-\mathfrak{p}\nabla u^{\circ}_{h},\tau_{h}-\mathfrak{p}\nabla v_{h})_{\Omega}+(\text{div}\,\sigma^{\circ}_{h},\text{div}\,\tau_{h})_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{p}\nabla w_{h}-z_{h}+f_{1},\tau_{h}-\mathfrak{p}\nabla v_{h})_{\Omega}-(f_{2},\text{div}\,\tau_{h})_{\Omega},\quad\forall\,[\tau_{h},v_{h}]\in\mathbb{H}_{h}.

(53)

Here the auxiliary variables
w_{h}\in\text{CG}_{m}
and
q_{h}\in\text{CG}^{\circ}_{m}
are conforming Galerkin approximations of the lifts
w
and
q
from (
17
) and (
20
), respectively, on the same mesh and with the same polynomial degree
m
as
u_{h}^{\circ}
; we set
z_{h}:=\nabla q_{h}
. The FE loss is assembled with these discrete lifts
(w_{h},z_{h})
. Note that the solution of the Galerkin problem (
53
) satisfies the minimal residual property

[\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{[\tau_{h},v_{h}]\in\mathbb{H}_{h}}\mathcal{L}([\tau_{h},v_{h}];\mathfrak{p}).

(54)

By construction,
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u_{h}^{\circ}]
minimizes the fiber loss over
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
, i.e., it is the orthogonal projection in the FOSLS inner product induced by
(\tau-\mathfrak{p}\nabla v,\eta-\mathfrak{p}\nabla s)+({\rm div\,}\tau,{\rm div\,}\eta)
.

Under standard regularity assumptions for the exact fiber solution
[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]
and uniform ellipticity of the parameter
\mathfrak{p}
, the FE loss evaluated at the FE solution
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u_{h}^{\circ}]
satisfies the following properties; a proof is given in
C.1
.

Theorem 1
.

Assume
\Omega
is Lipschitz and
\mathfrak{p}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
is uniformly bounded with
0<\alpha\leq\mathfrak{p}(x)\leq\beta<\infty
independent of
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
. Let
\Sigma_{h}=\mathrm{RT}^{\circ}_{k}
and
\mathbb{U}_{h}=\mathrm{CG}^{\circ}_{m}
on a shape-regular mesh of size
h
with
k\geq 0
,
m\geq 1
. Let
[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]
be the exact solution and
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}]
the Galerkin solution in
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
. Then, for each fixed
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, one has the error-residual equivalence

\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\|\sigma^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ}_{h}\|_{H({\rm div\,};\Omega)}^{2}+\|u^{\circ}-u^{\circ}_{h}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2},

(55)

with equivalence constants depending only on
\alpha,\beta
and the domain.

Moreover, if
\sigma^{\circ}\in H^{s_{\sigma}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
{\rm div\,}\sigma^{\circ}\in H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}(\Omega)
with
s_{\sigma},s_{{\rm div\,}}\geq 0
, and
u^{\circ}\in H^{s_{u}}(\Omega)
with
s_{u}\geq 1
, then

\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2\min(k+1,s_{\sigma})}\,\|\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{\sigma}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(k+1,s_{{\rm div\,}})}\,\|{\rm div\,}\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(m,s_{u}-1)}\,\|u^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{u}}}^{2}.

(56)

In particular, if
\sigma^{\circ}\in H^{k+1}
,
{\rm div\,}\sigma^{\circ}\in H^{k+1}
, and
u^{\circ}\in H^{m+1}
, then

\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}+h^{2m}
, and the balanced choice
m=k+1
yields the optimal scaling

\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}
.

4.2  Conforming finite element approximation of the elasticity problem

For the elasticity problem and each parameter
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, we approximate the lifted fiber solution
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]
by a conforming Galerkin FOSLS discretization in the mixed FE space

\mathbb{H}_{h}=\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}\subset H_{0,\Gamma_{N}}({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where we take

\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}=(\mathrm{RT}_{k}^{\circ})^{d}\times(\mathrm{CG}_{m}^{\circ})^{d},\quad k\geq 0,\;m\geq 1.

Note the essential boundary conditions are built into the spaces:
\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h}\cdot\hbox{\hskip 3.00117pt\hskip-3.00117pt\hbox{$n$}\hskip-3.00117pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.00117pt}=\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$0$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}
on
\Gamma_{N}
for
\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h}\in\Sigma_{h}
and
\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}=\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$0$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}
on
\Gamma_{D}
for
\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}\in\mathbb{U}_{h}
.
We parametrize the FE approximations
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]
in the same form as in (
51
) with the tensor- and vector-valued basis functions of
\Sigma_{h}
and
\mathbb{U}_{h}
, respectively, with corresponding coefficient vectors
\bm{\sigma}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})
and
\bm{u}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})
.
Discrete boundary lifts are computed analogously to the diffusion case. Specifically, we compute
\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}_{h}\in(\mathrm{CG}_{m})^{d}
as the vector-valued harmonic lift of the Dirichlet data by solving the auxiliary problem (
39
), and
\hbox{\hskip 2.41145pt\hskip-2.41145pt\hbox{$q$}\hskip-2.41145pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.41145pt}_{h}\in(\mathrm{CG}^{\circ}_{m})^{d}
as the solution of the auxiliary problem (
42
); we then set the tensor lift
\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h}:=\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{${\mathrm{grad}\,}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.44444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.41145pt\hskip-2.41145pt\hbox{$q$}\hskip-2.41145pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.41145pt}_{h}
. The FE loss and right-hand side are assembled with these discrete lifts
(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h})
.

For each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, we compute the FE solution
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
by solving the Galerkin system

\displaystyle({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}],\,{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}])_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=([{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}_{h})-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\hskip-2.98613pt\hbox{$f$}\hskip-2.98613pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.98613pt}],\,{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}])_{\Omega},\quad\forall[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h},

(57)

which in expanded form reads: find
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
such that

\displaystyle\quad({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}(\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h})),\,{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}(\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h}-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h})))_{\Omega}+(\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\,\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h})_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}_{h})-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h},\,{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}(\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h}-{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h})))_{\Omega}-(\hbox{\hskip 2.98613pt\hskip-2.98613pt\hbox{$f$}\hskip-2.98613pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.98613pt},\,\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h})_{\Omega},\quad\forall[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}.

(58)

Equivalently,
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]
minimizes the FE fiber loss over
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
, i.e.,

[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{[\hbox{\hskip 1.84393pt\hskip-1.84393pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 1.84393pt\hskip-1.84393pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.84393pt}$}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.84393pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.1256pt\hskip-2.1256pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.1256pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.1256pt}_{h}]\in\mathbb{H}_{h}}\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}];\mathfrak{p}).

(59)

Note that the Cauchy stress tensor

\sigma

in (
35
) is symmetric, while the FE approximation
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}_{h}=\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}+\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h}
, with
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}\in(\text{RT}_{k}^{\circ})^{d}
and
\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.54515pt\hskip-2.54515pt\hbox{$z$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}$}\hskip-2.54515pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.54515pt}_{h}=\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{${\mathrm{grad}\,}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.44444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.41145pt\hskip-2.41145pt\hbox{$q$}\hskip-2.41145pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.41145pt}_{h}
with
\hbox{\hskip 2.41145pt\hskip-2.41145pt\hbox{$q$}\hskip-2.41145pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.41145pt}_{h}\in(\text{CG}_{m}^{\circ})^{d}
, is not necessarily symmetric. To address this, we can impose weak symmetry by adding a penalty term
\|\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}_{h}-\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}_{h}^{\top}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}
to the FE loss, leading to corresponding modifications in the Galerkin system (
57
). We omit this for simplicity.

Under standard regularity assumptions for the exact fiber solution
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]
and uniform bounds for the elasticity tensor
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the FE loss evaluated at the FE solution satisfies similar properties as in Theorem
1
. We present the theorem and proof in
C.2
for completeness.

5  Reduced basis neural operators

5.1  Reduced basis approximations

Given the high-dimensional nature of the FE coefficient vectors
{\bm{\sigma}}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{\sigma}}
and
{\bm{u}}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{u}}
in (
51
), directly parameterizing these full DoF representations with a neural network leads to very large models and expensive residual evaluations, especially for large-scale FE discretizations. Instead, we seek a low-dimensional representation of the parametric solution manifold that is conformal with the underlying variational structure and allows for efficient evaluation of the loss.

The following facts and notational conventions will be frequently used in what follows.
For the parametric coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})=({\bm{\sigma}}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p});{\bm{u}}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}}
of the FE functions
s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]
in the diffusion case, we first equip
{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}}
with the discrete
H({\mathrm{div}\,})\times H^{1}
-norm

||{\bm{s}}_{h}||_{{X_{h}}}^{2}:=\|[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}]\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{h}}=(\sigma^{\circ}_{h},\sigma^{\circ}_{h})_{H({\mathrm{div}\,})}+(u^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h})_{H^{1}},

(60)

where each term is assembled using the same FE pair as in the high-fidelity discretization. The corresponding Gram matrix
{X_{h}}
defines the discrete norm
||{\bm{s}}_{h}||_{{X_{h}}}^{2}={\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}{X_{h}}{\bm{s}}_{h}
.
We then perform Proper Orthogonal Decomposition (POD) in this
{X_{h}}
-inner product. Specifically, given
N_{s}
snapshot solutions for a training sample set
\mathfrak{P}_{N_{s}}=\{\mathfrak{p}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{N_{s}}\}\subset\mathfrak{P}
, stored in the matrix
\mathbf{S}=\mathbf{S}_{\mathfrak{P}_{N_{s}}}=({\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}_{1}),\dots,{\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}_{N_{s}}))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}\times N_{s}}
,
we solve the eigenvalue problem

\mathbf{C}{\bm{v}}_{k}=\lambda_{k}{\bm{v}}_{k},\quad k=1,\dots,N_{s},

(61)

where
\mathbf{C}:=\mathbf{S}^{\top}{X_{h}}\mathbf{S}/N_{s}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{s}\times N_{s}}
. The eigenpairs
(\lambda_{k},{\bm{v}}_{k})
, with
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots
, yield the POD basis vectors
\pi_{k}=\mathbf{S}{\bm{v}}_{k}/\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}}
,
k=1,\dots,N_{s}
, which are orthonormal with respect to the
{X_{h}}
-inner product; see
[
51
, Chapter 6]
.

The functions
\phi_{k}\in\mathbb{H}_{h}
,
k=1,\ldots,r
, whose FE coefficients are given by the POD basis vectors
\pi_{k}
, thus form an
\mathbb{H}
-orthonormal basis
of an
r
-dimensional subspace
\mathbb{H}_{r}=\text{span}\{\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{r}\}
, which we refer to as the POD-RB subspace of
\mathbb{H}_{h}
. Hence, the matrix
\Pi_{r}=(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{r})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N^{s}_{h}\times r}
with columns
\pi_{k}
, defines for any
{\bm{s}}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}}
via
{\bm{s}}_{r}=\Pi_{r}^{\top}{X_{h}}{\bm{s}}_{h}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
a projection onto an
r
-dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}}
. Specifically,

s_{r}=\Phi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r},\text{ with }\Phi_{r}=(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{r})\text{ and }{\bm{s}}_{r}=\Pi_{r}^{\top}{X_{h}}{\bm{s}}_{h}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r},

is the
\mathbb{H}
-orthogonal projection of the FE function
s_{h}
with coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{h}
. Conversely, given a coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
of a function
s_{r}
in
\mathbb{H}_{r}
, the coefficient vector of
s_{h}
is given by

{\bm{s}}_{h}=\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}}.

(62)

In practice, we take the projection dimension
r
such that
51

||s_{h}-s_{r}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{P};\mathbb{H}_{h})}=||{\bm{s}}_{h}-\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}||_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{P};{X_{h}})}^{2}\approx\sum_{k=r+1}^{N_{s}}\lambda_{k}\leq\tau\sum_{k=1}^{N_{s}}\lambda_{k},

(63)

for a desired tolerance
\tau\ll 1
. Equivalently,
s_{r}
approximates the “truth-space” solution

s_{h}
in
\mathbb{H}_{h}
with relative error
\sqrt{\tau}
.
When the solution manifold has rapidly decaying Kolmogorov
r
-widths, we have
r\ll N_{h}^{s}
.

We will learn reduced basis neural operator (RBNO)
surrogates

s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)=[\sigma^{\circ}_{r},u^{\circ}_{r}](\mathfrak{p};\theta)\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
to the parameter-to-solution map
\mathfrak{p}\mapsto s(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]\in\mathbb{H}
in terms of elements from the
hypothesis class

\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta):=\Big\{s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)=\Phi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta),\;{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r},\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P},\theta\in\Theta\Big\}\subset L_{\mu}^{2}(\mathfrak{P},\mathbb{H}_{r}),

(64)

where the expansion coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)
is represented by neural networks with input variables
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
and trainable parameters
\theta\in\Theta
in a suitable parameter space
\Theta
.

On account of FOSLS stability in (
111
) and (
117
), the loss
\mathcal{L}

quantifies the error

\|s(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\eqsim\mathcal{L}(s_{r};\mathfrak{p})=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}s_{r}-S\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

uniformly in
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, where
S
is the source term, e.g.,
S=[\mathfrak{p}\nabla w-z+f_{1},f_{2}]
for the diffusion problem.

In order to see how errors of this type depend on the two constituents
\mathbb{H}_{h},\mathbb{H}_{r}
, we derive first corresponding decompositions of the loss. To that end, we make standard use of the fact that loss minimizers over any subspace of
\mathbb{H}
are solutions of the normal equation posed on this subspace (with source data projected accordingly) and hence enjoy Galerkin orthogonality.

Lemma 1
.

Assume that
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
is a closed subspace of
\mathbb{H}
and that
\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})=[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),\tilde{u}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
solves

({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p}),{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}^{\prime})_{\Omega}=(S,{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}^{\prime})_{\Omega},\quad\forall\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}.

(65)

Then we have

\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}^{\prime};\mathfrak{p})=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p}))\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-S\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}},\quad\forall\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}.

(66)

In other words,

\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}}\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}^{\prime};\mathfrak{p})=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}}\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p}))\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}.

(67)

Proof.

By definition, we have for the exact FOSLS solution
s(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]\in\mathbb{H}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s}^{\prime};\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}^{\prime}-S\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p}))+{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p}))\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p}))\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p}))\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})),{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})))_{\Omega}.

Since by (
65
),
({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})),{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{s}^{\prime})_{\Omega}=0
for all
\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
, and since
\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
, we conclude that
({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}^{\prime}-\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})),{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})))_{\Omega}=0
for all
\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbb{H}}
, whence the assertion follows.
∎

We are now in a position to interrelate best approximation errors for later purposes.

Theorem 2
.

As before, let
\mathbb{H}_{h}=\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}\subset H({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Omega)
be the
H({\rm div\,})\times H^{1}
-conforming FE space and, for each fixed parameter
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, let
s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]\in\mathbb{H}_{h}
be the FE FOSLS solution and

s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}_{r}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})]=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{[\tau_{r},v_{r}]\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\mathcal{L}([\tau_{r},v_{r}];\mathfrak{p})

(68)

be the RB FOSLS solution in the POD subspace
\mathbb{H}_{r}\subset\mathbb{H}_{h}
.
Then the following hold:

1.

For any
s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
, there holds

\mathcal{L}(s_{r};\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\|\,s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p}).

(69)

In addition, the reduced FOSLS minimizer
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
in (
68
) satisfies the quasi-optimality bound

\|\,s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}\eqsim\min_{t_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\|\,t_{r}-s(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}.

(70)

2.

For any
s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
, there holds

\mathcal{L}(s_{r};\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\|\,s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p}).

(71)

In addition, the reduced FOSLS minimizer
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
in (
68
)
satisfies the quasi-optimality bound

\|\,s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}\eqsim\min_{t_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\|\,t_{r}-s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}.

(72)

3.

The function
s_{r}\in\mathbb{X}_{r}:=L_{\mu}^{2}(\mathfrak{P};\mathbb{H}_{r})
that for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
solves (
65
) for

\tilde{\mathbb{H}}=\mathbb{H}_{r}
,
is the unique minimizer

s_{r}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{t\in\mathbb{X}_{r}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\mathcal{L}(t(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\big]=:\mathop{\rm argmin}_{t\in\mathbb{X}_{r}}\mathcal{L}(t;\mathfrak{P})

(73)

and

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\big]\eqsim\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\|s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\big]\eqsim\min_{t_{r}\in\mathbb{X}_{r}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\|t_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\big],

(74)

with a constant that depends only on
\mathfrak{P}
.

Proof.

1.

Taking
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}:=\mathbb{H}_{r}
, (
69
) follows from
(
66
) in Lemma
1
with
\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})=s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
and

\tilde{s}^{\prime}=s_{r}
, combined with FOSLS stability (
111
) and (
117
)
for the diffusion and elasticity model, respectively. We obtain (
70
) by the optimality of
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
and FOSLS stability, i.e., for any
t_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
,

\|\,s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}\eqsim\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-S\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}=\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\leq\mathcal{L}(t_{r};\mathfrak{p})=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}t_{r}-S\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\eqsim\|\,t_{r}-s(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}.

(75)

2.

The same reasoning for
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}=\mathbb{H}_{h}
,
\tilde{s}(\mathfrak{p})=[\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]
, confirms (
71
), noting that
s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
are also elements in
\mathbb{H}_{h}
. Applying minimization of
s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
over (
71
), we have

\min_{s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\mathcal{L}(s_{r};\mathfrak{p})\simeq\min_{s_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\|s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p}),

(76)

or equivalently,

\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\;\mathfrak{p})\simeq\min_{t_{r}\in\mathbb{H}_{r}}\|t_{r}-s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p}).

(77)

Then (
72
) holds when we combing (
77
) with
\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\|\,s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\,\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})
which is obtained by plugging
s_{r}=s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
in (
71
).

3.

This is an immediate consequence of 1. and uniform FOSLS stability (
111
) and (
117
).

∎

Remark 1
.

Theorem
2
says that
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
minimizes for each
\mathfrak{p}
the loss over
\mathbb{H}_{r}
and (near-)best approximates
s(\mathfrak{p})
.
Statement 2 says that
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
(near-)best approximates the FE-FOSLS solution from

\mathbb{H}_{r}
. Overall this states that the error incurred by
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})

with respect to the exact solution is uniformly proportional to the sum of the
best approximation errors of
s(\mathfrak{p})
from the FE space
\mathbb{H}_{h}
and
the error of the approximation of the FE-FOSLS solution by the RB-FOSLS solution.

Corollary 1
.

Adhering to the above assumptions,
let

s_{r}(\theta^{*})\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{t_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\mathcal{L}(t_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\big].

(78)

Then, with a proportionality constant, depending only on uniform FOSLS-stability
(
111
) and (
117
), there holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\|s(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\big]\lesssim h^{2\eta}+\sum_{j>r}\lambda_{j}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\|s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\Big],

(79)

where
\eta
depends on the polynomial orders of the FE approximation and the regularity of
s(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathbb{H}
,
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, as in Theorem
1
. Note that the last summand reflects the best approximation from the hypothesis class to the best approximation of the exact FOSLS solutions
s(\mathfrak{p})
from
\mathbb{H}_{r}
.

Proof.

We infer from Theorem
2
,
(
69
), that, with
s_{r}(\theta^{*})\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)
, and FOSLS stability (
111
) and (
117
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\|s(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\big]

\displaystyle\eqsim\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\|s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\Big]

\displaystyle\eqsim\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\|s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta^{*})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\|s_{h}(\mathfrak{p})-s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\Big],

(80)

where we have invoked Theorem
2
, Statement 2 in the last step.
Bounding the first term in the last inequality by (
63
), using again that, uniformly in

\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, on account of FOSLS-stability and the best approximation property of the FE-FOSLS solutions, we conclude that

\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\inf_{t_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathbb{H}_{h}}\|t_{h}(\mathfrak{p})-s(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}.

Finally, invoking the error bounds in
C.1
and
C.2
, finishes the proof.
∎

5.2  Loss evaluation

We learn an approximation to the optimal approximation
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
from the hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)\subset\mathbb{X}_{r}
(see (
64
)) by minimizing an empirical loss
over
\Theta
. Since this will require frequent evaluations of fiber losses
\mathcal{L}(\cdot;\mathfrak{p})
at randomly drawn samples
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, the cost of such evaluations is an issue. In fact, substituting the FE approximation (
51
) in the residual fiber loss function (
30
), we obtain

\mathcal{L}(s_{h}(\mathfrak{p});\mathfrak{p})={\bm{w}}(\mathfrak{p})^{\top}\,W_{\mathfrak{p}}\,{\bm{w}}(\mathfrak{p})={\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\circ}{\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})+2{\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}+\beta_{\mathfrak{p}},

(81)

where
{\bm{w}}(\mathfrak{p})=({\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p});1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}+1}
is a concatenation of the parametric coefficient vectors
{\bm{s}}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N_{h}^{s}}
and scalar
1
. Here
W_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(N_{h}^{s}+1)\times(N_{h}^{s}+1)}
is a sparse symmetric matrix assembled from the FE approximation at each parameter
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, which has the block structure

\displaystyle W_{\mathfrak{p}}=\begin{pmatrix}W^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}&{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\\[4.0pt]
{\bm{\alpha}}^{\top}_{\mathfrak{p}}&\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}\end{pmatrix},

with
W^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}\times N_{h}^{s}}
,
{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}}
, and
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{R}
assembled from the bilinear, linear, and constant forms in the loss function (
30
). Note that evaluation of the loss function (
81
) has complexity
O(N_{h}^{s})
when employing sparse matrix vector products, which is expensive for large-scale FE discretizations.

To reduce the computational complexity, we introduce a RB computation of the loss function (
81
), when evaluated at elements in the RB space
\mathbb{H}_{r}
. Let
s_{r}
denote the RB approximation of the FE solution
s_{h}
,
with corresponding approximation of the coefficient vectors by the projection in (
62
). Then the RB loss function can be evaluated as

\mathcal{L}(s_{r};\mathfrak{p})={\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})+2{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}+\beta_{\mathfrak{p}},

(82)

where reduced weights
W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}:=\Pi^{\top}_{r}W^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Pi_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
and vectors
{\bm{\alpha}}^{r}_{\mathfrak{p}}:=\Pi^{\top}_{r}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
can be precomputed and stored for the training samples of the parameter, allowing a fast evaluation of the loss function with complexity
O(r^{2})
. The RB FOSLS minimizer
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\in\mathbb{H}_{r}
from (
68
) can then be computed with the RB coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{r}
obtained by solving the reduced normal equation

W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}\,{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})=-{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}.

(83)

For the elasticity problem, we follow the same computation of the RB approximation as in the diffusion case. Specifically, we adopt the following discrete
H({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
-norm as in
46

||{\bm{s}}_{h}||_{{X_{h}}}^{2}:=(\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt},\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt})_{L^{2}}+(\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt},{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.44794pt\hskip-5.44794pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt}$}\hskip-5.44794pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.44794pt})_{L_{2}}+(\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 5.45316pt\hskip-5.45316pt\hbox{$u^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.45316pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.45316pt}),{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 5.45316pt\hskip-5.45316pt\hbox{$u^{\circ}_{h}$}\hskip-5.45316pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.45316pt}))_{L^{2}},

(84)

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}
are the stiffness tensor and its inverse at the mean of the parameter
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathfrak{P}
. The same statements as in Proposition
2
hold for the RB approximation of the elasticity problem with the loss function (
48
) and the discrete norm (
84
).

We conclude this section with one further prerequisite that will be needed in the next section.

Proposition 1
.

Assume
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}\subset L_{\infty}(\Omega)
is uniformly bounded as in Theorem
1
. Then for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, the reduced weights
W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}
, vectors
{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}
, and scalars
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
, defined in (
82
), satisfy the uniform bounds

\gamma_{-}I\preceq W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}\preceq\gamma_{+}I,\qquad\|{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{+}}\,S_{\max},\qquad|\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}|\leq S_{\max}^{2}.

Here
c\leq\gamma_{-}\leq\gamma_{+}\leq C
with the stability constants
0<c<C<\infty
in Lemma
3
, and
S_{\max}
is the uniform bound for the input source
\|S(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq S_{\max}
,
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
. For instance, for diffusion one has
S=[\mathfrak{p}\nabla w-z+f_{1},f_{2}]
.

Proof.

Since
W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}=\Pi_{r}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\circ}\,\Pi_{r}
, for any
{\bm{s}}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
we have

{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}\,=\,(\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r})^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\circ}(\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}).

Uniform FOSLS stability (see Lemma
3
) implies there exist
0<\gamma_{-}\leq\gamma_{+}<\infty
such that the stability estimates (
111
) hold in FE spaces
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}\subset\Sigma\times\mathbb{U}
with constants
c\leq\gamma_{-}
and
\gamma_{+}\leq C
, i.e., for all FE coefficient vectors
{\bm{s}}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N_{h}^{s}}
,

\gamma_{-}\,{\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}X_{h}{\bm{s}}_{h}\;\leq\;{\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\circ}{\bm{s}}_{h}\;\leq\;\gamma_{+}\,{\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}X_{h}{\bm{s}}_{h},\qquad\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}.

Therefore, by setting
{\bm{s}}_{h}=\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}
, there holds

\gamma_{-}\,{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}(\Pi_{r}^{\top}X_{h}\Pi_{r}){\bm{s}}_{r}\;\leq\;{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}\;\leq\;\gamma_{+}\,{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}(\Pi_{r}^{\top}X_{h}\Pi_{r}){\bm{s}}_{r}.

By the
{X_{h}}
-orthonormality of
\Pi_{r}
,
\Pi_{r}^{\top}X_{h}\Pi_{r}=I_{r}
, hence

\gamma_{-}\,\|{\bm{s}}_{r}\|_{2}^{2}\leq{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}\leq\gamma_{+}\,\|{\bm{s}}_{r}\|_{2}^{2}
, proving the spectral bounds.
Next, from the quadratic expansion
\mathcal{L}({\bm{s}}_{h};\mathfrak{p})={\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}W_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\circ}{\bm{s}}_{h}+2\,{\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}+\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the linear term obeys, for any FE coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{h}
with corresponding FE functions
s_{h}
,

|{\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}|\;=\;({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}s_{h},S(\mathfrak{p}))\;\leq\;\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}s_{h}\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,\|S(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\;\leq\;\sqrt{\gamma_{+}}\,\|S(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\,({\bm{s}}_{h}^{\top}X_{h}{\bm{s}}_{h})^{1/2},

where we have used Cauchy–Schwarz in the first inequality and the uniform stability bound in the second. Taking
{\bm{s}}_{h}=\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r}
with
\|{\bm{s}}_{r}\|_{2}=1
and using
{\bm{s}}_{r}^{\top}(\Pi_{r}^{\top}X_{h}\Pi_{r}){\bm{s}}_{r}=1
, yields

\|{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}\|_{2}=\sup_{\|{\bm{s}}_{r}\|_{2}=1}(\Pi_{r}{\bm{s}}_{r})^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{+}}\,\|S(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{+}}\,S_{\max}.

Finally,
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}=\|S(\mathfrak{p})\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq S_{\max}^{2}
. Uniformity follows from the hypotheses.
∎

5.3  Learning the reduced basis neural operator

Recall that the elements of the hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)
, defined in (
64
), belong for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, as functions of
x\in\Omega
, to the
r
–dimensional RB space constructed in
Section

5.1
. As indicated in
Section

2
, learning a parametric map will be based on minimizing an empirical risk of the type

\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\hat{R}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}(\theta),\quad\mbox{where}\quad\hat{R}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}(\theta):=\hat{R}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}(s_{r}(\cdot;\theta)):=\frac{1}{\#{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{p}\in{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}}\mathcal{L}\big(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta);\mathfrak{p}\big).

(85)

In what follows

{\widehat{\mathfrak{P}}}=\{\mathfrak{p}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{N}\}\subset\mathfrak{P},

stands for a collection of finite i.i.d. random samples from
\mathfrak{P}
. In computations, the evaluation of the RB loss
\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta);\mathfrak{p})
is always based on the right hand side expression (
82
) for
{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})={\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)
. The coefficients in
{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta)
need to approximate for every
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
the coefficient vector
{\bm{s}}_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
of function
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
. Recall that those are obtained when minimizing the “ideal continuous” loss which we also abbreviate, for convenience, as follows: for any
\theta\in\Theta
we write
s_{r}(\cdot;\theta)=[\sigma^{\circ}_{r}(\cdot;\theta),u^{\circ}_{r}(\cdot;\theta)]\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)
and set

R(s_{r}(\cdot;\theta))=:R(\theta):=\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\Big[\mathcal{L}(s_{r}(\mathfrak{p};\theta);\mathfrak{p})\Big].

We refer to functions
s_{r}(\cdot;\theta)=[\sigma^{\circ}_{r}(\cdot;\theta),u^{\circ}_{r}(\cdot;\theta)]\in\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)
as RBNO approximations to the solution of the RB-FOSLS problem.

In order to estimate the accuracy of empirical loss minimizers with respect to the Bochner norm
\|w\|^{2}_{\mathbb{X}}=\int_{\mathfrak{P}}\|w(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}d\mu(\mathfrak{p})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{p}\sim\mu}\big[\|w(\mathfrak{p})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\big]
, we combine the previous error bounds with (rather standard) concepts from Learning Theory. Here we are content with a relatively simple version that may not be the best possible. Nevertheless, their applicability imposes some conditions on the hypothesis class
\mathcal{H}_{r}(\Theta)
that need to be ensured.

Boundedness:
Since
s_{r}(\mathfrak{p})
minimizes the (ideal) loss over
