2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}
.

Lemma 4
.

Assume the stiffness tensor
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is uniformly symmetric positive definite (SPD) with bounds independent of
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, i.e., there exist positive constants
c_{0},c_{1}
such that

c_{0}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}):\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt})\leq\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}):{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt})\leq c_{1}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}):\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt})\quad\forall\,\hbox{\hskip 3.71413pt\hskip-3.71413pt\hbox{$w$}\hskip-3.71413pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.71413pt}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then for all
[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\in H_{0,\Gamma_{N}}({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

c\,\big(\|\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{H({\mathrm{div}\,})}^{2}+\|\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)\leq\|[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2}\leq C\,\big(\|\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{H({\mathrm{div}\,})}^{2}+\|\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big),

(117)

with constants
c,C>0
depending only on
c_{0},c_{1}
and the domain. Moreover,
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an isomorphism from
\mathbb{H}=H_{0,\Gamma_{N}}({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
onto
\mathbb{L}_{2}=L_{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times L_{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.

Let
c_{0},c_{1}>0
be the uniform SPD bounds for
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and let
C_{P}=C_{P}(\Omega,\Gamma_{D})
and
C_{K}=C_{K}(\Omega,\Gamma_{D})
be the Poincaré and Korn constants so that
\|\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{P}\|\nabla\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{L^{2}}
and
\|\nabla\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{K}\|\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt})\|_{L^{2}}
for all
\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\in H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
(e.g.,
11
).
The following arguments are analogous to the diffusion case, beginning with (
117
).

Upper bound.

Triangle- and Cauchy Schwarz inequalities provide as before

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1/2}\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{L_{2}}+\|{\mathcal{C}}^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}))\|_{L_{2}}+\|\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{L_{2}}\leq c_{0}^{-1}\sqrt{2}\|\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{H({\rm div\,})}+c_{1}\|\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{${\mathrm{grad}\,}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.44444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt})\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(2c_{0}+c_{1})^{1/2}\|[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{H({\rm div\,})\times H^{1}},

confirming the upper bound in (
117
).

Lower bound.

Along the same lines as in the previous section, one can prove

\|\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt})\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\frac{C_{P}}{c_{0}^{1/2}}\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}},

(and likewise for
\|\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 10.56944pt\hskip-10.56944pt\hbox{${\mathrm{grad}\,}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-3.94444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}$}\hskip-10.56944pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.44444pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 10.56944pt}{(v)}\|_{L_{2}}
) with an absolute proportionality constant to conclude further

\|\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\frac{C_{K}C_{P}}{c_{0}^{1/2}}\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}},\quad\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}\in\mathbb{U}:=H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}),

as well as

\|\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}\|_{H({\rm div\,})}\lesssim c_{1}^{1/2}\Big(1+\frac{c_{1}^{1/2}C_{K}C_{P}}{c_{0}^{1/2}}\Big)\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt},\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}.

Assembling these bounds confirms the lower bound as well with an analogous dependence of
c
on

(c_{1}/c_{0})
, describing the square of the condition number of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

While (
117
) so far establishes bijectivity of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as a mapping from
\mathbb{H}
to its range
in
\mathbb{L}_{2}
, the same argument as in the previous section shows that this range is indeed all of
\mathbb{L}_{2}
,
which by the Inverse Mapping Theorem completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Error estimates for finite element approximations

C.1  Proof of
Theorem

1
for the stationary diffusion problem

Proof.

Again, for the convenience of the reader, we recall some relevant facts that can be found, for instance,
in
11
concerning the FE error estimate for the first-order system least-squares (FOSLS) formulation of the diffusion problem. Recall the fiber-residual loss from (
30
)

\mathcal{L}([\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p}):=\|\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}-(\mathfrak{p}\nabla\tilde{u}^{\circ}+\mathfrak{p}\nabla w-z+f_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|{\rm div\,}\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}+f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Let
S:=[\mathfrak{p}\nabla w-z+f_{1},\,f_{2}]
denote the source vector. Then

\mathcal{L}([\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p})=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}]-S\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d+1})}^{2}=\|\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}-(\mathfrak{p}\nabla\tilde{u}^{\circ}+\mathfrak{p}\nabla w-z+f_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|{\rm div\,}\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}+f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Let
[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]=[\sigma^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]
denote the exact fiber solution, which satisfies
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]=S
so that
\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p})=0
(hence is the unique minimizer of the loss). For conforming FE spaces
\Sigma_{h}\subset H_{0,\Gamma_{N}}({\rm div\,};\Omega)
and
\mathbb{U}_{h}\subset H^{1}_{0,\Gamma_{D}}(\Omega)
, the Galerkin FOSLS solution
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}]=[\sigma^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),u^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
minimizes for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
,
\mathcal{L}(\cdot;\mathfrak{p})
over
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
and satisfies the corresponding normal equations in
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
.

Since
[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]
solves the normal equation,
one also has for any
[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}]\in\mathbb{H}

\mathcal{L}([\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}];\mathfrak{p})=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}]-{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}=\|{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{p}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}-u^{\circ}]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}=\|[\tilde{\sigma}^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ},\tilde{u}^{\circ}-u^{\circ}]\|^{2}_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

Since
[\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}]
solves the normal equations in
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
, Galerkin orthogonality with
respect to the inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and the resulting best-approximation property
yields

\|[\sigma^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}-u^{\circ}_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}=\min_{[\tau_{h},v_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}}\|[\sigma^{\circ}-\tau_{h},u^{\circ}-v_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

In what follows, to simplify notation, we occasionally write
L_{2}
without specifying the domain and range, when this is clear from the context.
Let
\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}:H({\rm div\,};\Omega)\to\Sigma_{h}
denote the canonical Raviart–Thomas interpolant,
(see
11
, section 2.5.2, page 109)
and
I_{m}:H^{1}(\Omega)\to\mathbb{U}_{h}
an
H^{1}
quasi-interpolant.
Choosing
[\tau_{h},v_{h}]=[\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ},I_{m}u^{\circ}]
and expanding the
\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-norm, yields

\displaystyle\|[\sigma^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}-u^{\circ}_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|[\sigma^{\circ}-\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}-I_{m}u^{\circ}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma^{\circ}-\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ}\|_{L_{2}}+\beta\|\nabla(u^{\circ}-I_{m}u^{\circ})\|_{L_{2}}+\|{\rm div\,}(\sigma^{\circ}-\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ})\|_{L_{2}}.

Standard RT and
H^{1}
interpolation estimates on shape-regular meshes (see, e.g.,
[
11
, Proposition 2.5.4]
) give, assuming regularity-orders
s_{\sigma},s_{{\rm div\,}}\geq 0
and
s_{u}\geq 1
for
\sigma^{\circ},{\rm div\,}\,\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ}
,
respectively,

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{\circ}-\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ}\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{\min(k+1,s_{\sigma})}\,\|\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{\sigma}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}(\sigma^{\circ}-\Pi^{\rm RT}_{k}\sigma^{\circ})\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{\min(k+1,s_{{\rm div\,}})}\,\|{\rm div\,}\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u^{\circ}-I_{m}u^{\circ})\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{\min(m,s_{u}-1)}\,\|u^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{u}}(\Omega)}.

where, generally
s_{\rm div\,}=s_{\sigma}-1
. Note that these estimates are therefore relevant when
s_{\sigma}-1
is at least
k+1
, which takes advantage of the particular properties of the Raviart-Thomas finite element spaces.

Combining these bounds, using
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\eqsim\|\cdot\|_{H({\rm div\,})\times H^{1}}
, we obtain the a priori bound stated in
Theorem

1
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})

\displaystyle=\|[\sigma^{\circ}-\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}-u^{\circ}_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2\min(k+1,s_{\sigma})}\,\|\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{\sigma}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(k+1,s_{{\rm div\,}})}\,\|{\rm div\,}\sigma^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(m,s_{u}-1)}\,\|u^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{u}}}^{2}.

In particular, when
s_{\sigma},s_{{\rm div\,}}\geq k+1
, and
s_{u}\geq m+1
, it reduces to

\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}+h^{2m}
. Thus, choosing
m=k+1
balances the contributions, yielding
\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}
.
∎

Remark 3

(approximate lifts consistency)
.

In practice, in the evaluation of the FE loss
\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})
, we replace the variational lifts
(w,z)
by their
\text{CG}_{m}
approximations
(w_{h},z_{h})
, leading to the approximate FE loss
\mathcal{L}_{h}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})
. It is straightforward to show (by
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
) that

\mathcal{L}_{h}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\leq 2\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})+2||\delta||_{L^{2}}^{2}\text{ and }\mathcal{L}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\leq 2\mathcal{L}_{h}([\sigma^{\circ}_{h},u^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})+2||\delta||_{L^{2}}^{2},

where the error
\delta:=\mathfrak{p}\nabla(w-w_{h})-(z-z_{h})
satisfies
\|\delta\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim h^{2m}

[
11
, Proposition 2.5.4]
) as the lifts are harmonic. This confirms that the approximate lifts do not degrade the asymptotic accuracy with
m\geq k+1
.

C.2  Error estimates of FE approximations for linear elasticity

Theorem 4
.

Assume
\Omega
is Lipschitz and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is uniformly bounded with bounds independent of
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
. Let
\Sigma_{h}=(\mathrm{RT}_{k}^{\circ})^{d}
and
\mathbb{U}_{h}=(\mathrm{CG}_{m}^{\circ})^{d}
on a shape-regular mesh of size
h
, with
k\geq 0
,
m\geq 1
. Let
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]
be the exact solution and
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]
the Galerkin solution in
\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}
. Then, for each fixed
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{P}
, and
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{p})]=[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}]
,
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p}),\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}(\mathfrak{p})]=[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]
,
one has the error–residual equivalence

\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\eqsim\|\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h}\|_{H({\mathrm{div}\,};\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})}^{2}+\|\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(118)

with equivalence constants, depending only on the uniform SPD bounds for
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and the domain.

Moreover, if
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\in H^{s_{\sigma}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
,
\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\in H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
s_{\sigma},s_{{\rm div\,}}\geq 0
, and
\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}\in H^{s_{u}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
s_{u}\geq 1
, then

\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2\min(k+1,s_{\sigma})}\,\|\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{\sigma}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(k+1,s_{{\rm div\,}})}\,\|\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{{\rm div\,}}}}^{2}+h^{2\min(m,s_{u}-1)}\,\|\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}\|_{H^{s_{u}}}^{2}.

(119)

In particular, if
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\in H^{k+1}
,
\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\,\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\in H^{k+1}
, and
\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}\in H^{m+1}
, then

\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}+h^{2m}
, and the balanced choice
m=k+1
yields the optimal scaling

\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}
.

Proof.

By the same orthogonal projection argument in
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
defined in
B.2
, the Galerkin solution
[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]
satisfies the best-approximation property

\|[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{P}}}=\min_{[\hbox{\hskip 1.84393pt\hskip-1.84393pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 1.84393pt\hskip-1.84393pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.84393pt}$}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 1.84393pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.1256pt\hskip-2.1256pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.1256pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{2.1097pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.1256pt}_{h}]\in\Sigma_{h}\times\mathbb{U}_{h}}\|[\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.18576pt\hskip-2.18576pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}$}\hskip-2.18576pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.18576pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}-\hbox{\hskip 2.603pt\hskip-2.603pt\hbox{$v$}\hskip-2.603pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.603pt}_{h}]\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

Choosing interpolation operators
\Pi_{h}
for
H({\mathrm{div}\,})
-conforming tensor fields and
I_{h}
for
H^{1}
-conforming vector fields with standard approximation properties, and using the stability lemma, we obtain the a priori bound (
119
) whenever
\Sigma_{h}
affords order-
k+1
approximation in
L_{2}
for tensors and their divergences, and
\mathbb{U}_{h}
affords order-
m
in
H^{1}
. This holds, for instance, for componentwise RT
k
spaces for stresses and CG
m
for displacements; see
[
11
, Ch. 2]
. Balancing
m=k+1
yields the optimal rate
\mathcal{L}([\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}_{h},\hbox{\hskip 2.86229pt\hskip-2.86229pt\hbox{$u$}\hskip-2.86229pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.86229pt}^{\circ}_{h}];\mathfrak{p})\lesssim h^{2(k+1)}
.
∎

Appendix D
Experimental details

D.1  Visualization of the sparsity pattern of weight

In
Figure

10
, we mark by blue dots the entries of the weight
W_{\mathfrak{p}}
whose magnitudes exceed
10^{-10}
. We observe that the nonzero entries are predominantly concentrated along and near the main diagonal. In addition to this banded structure, finer diagonal patterns offset from the main diagonal are visible. These arise because each finite element contributes a structured local stencil, resulting in repeated small dense sub-blocks in the global matrix.

(a)

Heat conduction: full

(b)

Heat conduction: 10,000–12,000

(c)

Heat conduction: 10,000–10,100

(d)

Darcy flow: full

(e)

Darcy flow: 10,000–12,000

(f)

Darcy flow: 10,000–10,100

(g)

Linear elasticity: full

(h)

Linear elasticity: 10,000–12,000

(i)

Linear elasticity: 10,000–10,100

Figure 10
:
Sparsity patterns of the weights
W_{\mathfrak{p}}
(threshold at
10^{-10}
) shown at three zoom levels: first column – full matrix
W_{\mathfrak{p}}
; second column – submatrix
W_{\mathfrak{p}}[10000:12000,10000:12000]
(highlighted by the red patch in the first column); third column – submatrix
W_{\mathfrak{p}}[10000:10100,10000:10100]
(highlighted by the red patch in the second column). Results are presented for three problem setups: heat conduction (top row), Darcy flow (middle row), and linear elasticity (bottom row).

D.2  Visualization of reduced weight

In
Figure

D.3  Architecture and training details

We report the configurations of network architectures and training details of RBNO, PCA-Net and FNO in
Tables

3
,
4
,
4
and
5
along with training time and inference time in
Table

6
. For the PCA-Net and FNO, pointwise evaluations on grid points of high-fidelity solutions are used as training labels:
500
solutions for heat conduction, and
1000
for Darcy flow and linear elasticity. These numbers match those used to construct the POD basis in RBNO, which is the only stage where RBNO accesses high-fidelity solutions. All models are trained for
3000
iterations for diffusion and
6000
iterations for elasticity, with a batch size that fits in GPU memory. As in RBNO, training and validation losses are monitored at each iteration, and the model weights corresponding to the lowest validation loss are saved.

D.4
H({\mathrm{div}\,})\times H^{1}
reconstruction from CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
representations

Starting from a pointwise evaluation of the approximate solution
(\hat{\sigma}^{\circ},\hat{u}^{\circ})
and its FE representation with CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
elements, we construct an
H(\mathrm{div})\times H^{1}
-conforming approximation by projecting into the space
\mathbb{H}_{h}=
RT
{}^{\circ}_{1}\times
CG
{}^{\circ}_{2}
in the
H(\mathrm{div})\times H^{1}
norm, enforcing the appropriate homogeneous boundary conditions.

For the diffusion problems, we solve for
(\sigma^{\circ},u^{\circ})\in\mathbb{H}_{h}
:

\displaystyle\quad(\sigma^{\circ},\tau)_{\Omega}+({\rm div\,}{\sigma^{\circ}},{\rm div\,}{\tau})_{\Omega}+(u^{\circ},v)_{\Omega}+({\mathrm{grad}\,}{u^{\circ}},{\mathrm{grad}\,}{v})_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=(\hat{\sigma}^{\circ},\tau)_{\Omega}+({\rm div\,}{\hat{\sigma}^{\circ}},{\rm div\,}{\tau})_{\Omega}+(\hat{u}^{\circ},v)_{\Omega}+({\mathrm{grad}\,}{\hat{u}^{\circ}},{\mathrm{grad}\,}{v})_{\Omega},\quad(\tau,v)\in\mathbb{H}_{h},

with
\sigma^{\circ}\cdot n=0
on
\Gamma_{N}
and
u^{\circ}=0
on
\Gamma_{D}
.

For the linear elasticity problem, we solve for
(\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt},\hbox{\hskip 5.1609pt\hskip-5.1609pt\hbox{$u^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.1609pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.1609pt})\in\mathbb{H}_{h}=(\text{RT}^{\circ}_{1})^{2}\times(\text{CG}^{\circ}_{2})^{2}
:

\displaystyle\quad(\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt},\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 7.63889pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48438pt\hskip-4.48438pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.48438pt\hskip-4.48438pt\hbox{$\tau^{\circ}$}\hskip-4.48438pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 4.48438pt}$}\hskip-4.48438pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 4.48438pt})_{\Omega}+(\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.15567pt\hskip-5.15567pt\hbox{$\sigma^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt}$}\hskip-5.15567pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.15567pt},{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\hbox{\hskip 4.48438pt\hskip-4.48438pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.48438pt\hskip-4.48438pt\hbox{$\tau^{\circ}$}\hskip-4.48438pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 4.48438pt}$}\hskip-4.48438pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 4.48438pt})_{\Omega}+(\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\varepsilon$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}$}\hskip-2.33159pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 5.1609pt\hskip-5.1609pt\hbox{$u^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.1609pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.1609pt}),{\mathcal{C}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.33159pt\hskip-2.33159pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.33159pt}(\hbox{\hskip 4.90161pt\hskip-4.90161pt\hbox{$v^{\circ}$}\hskip-4.90161pt\hskip \displaystyle=(\hbox{\hskip 7.63889pt\hskip-7.63889pt\hbox{${\mathrm{div}\,}$}\hskip-7.63889pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip with
\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.85706pt\hskip-2.85706pt\hbox{$\sigma$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}$}\hskip-2.85706pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.85706pt}^{\circ}\cdot\hbox{\hskip 3.00117pt\hskip-3.00117pt\hbox{$n$}\hskip-3.00117pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 3.00117pt}=(0,0)^{\top}

and
\hbox{\hskip 5.1609pt\hskip-5.1609pt\hbox{$u^{\circ}$}\hskip-5.1609pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rule{3.01385pt}{0.6pt}$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.1609pt}=(0,0)^{\top}

.

; the qualitative conclusions are unchanged.

D.5  Additional results (expansion of
Table

2
)

In addition to the results shown in
Table

2
, we consider training with an MSE loss that matches the neural network predictions of the RB coefficients to their corresponding labels; this approach is denoted as RBNO (RB coef.). We also investigate whether adding this loss to the original RBNO training loss (the residual loss) during training can further reduce the error and loss over the test samples; this variant is denoted as RBNO (both).

For PCA-Net and FNO, the networks are trained to match pointwise evaluations at grid points to the corresponding labels and output pointwise approximations at test time. To compare these approximations with the high-fidelity solution, we propose two approaches. First, we construct a CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
representation of the approximation by mapping the pointwise evaluations to the degrees of freedom of the CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
function. The first comparison method then evaluates this approximation against the CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
representation of the high-fidelity solution (obtained by evaluating the solution on grid points and mapping to CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
degrees of freedom). In the second method, we project the CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
approximation into the RT
{}_{1}\times
CG
2
space in the
\mathbb{H}=H({\mathrm{div}\,})\times H^{1}
-norm and compare it with the high-fidelity solution (in the RT
{}_{1}\times
CG
2
FE space). We indicate which reference function space is used, [CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
] or [RT
{}_{1}\times
CG
2
], in the results. The results for PCA-Net and FNO in table
Table

2
are now denoted as PCA-Net [CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
] and FNO [CG
{}_{1}\times
CG
1
]. The residual losses are evaluated using the corresponding FE representations of the approximations. In addition, we train PCA-Net to match the RB coefficients, rather than the PCA coefficients computed from solution evaluations at grid points. In this variant, we use the same basis as in RBNO to approximate the solution; this approach is denoted as PCA-Net (RB coef.).

We can see that RBNO with residual loss provides the best overall performance in terms of approximation accuracy and residual loss across all three problems. Adding the RB-coefficient MSE loss yields slight improvements for the diffusion problem, but in the elasticity problem, it even degrades performance. Standard data-driven models based on pointwise evaluations (FNO, PCA-Net) perform reasonably in
\mathbb{L}
-norm, but struggle with
\mathbb{H}
-norm and physics consistency (indicated by residual loss). While the PCA-Net trained on RB coefficient data produces a slightly larger
\mathbb{L}
-norm error than its pointwise counterpart (except for the heat conduction problem, where it is even lower), it generally achieves lower residual loss and
\mathbb{H}
-norm error (except for the
\mathbb{H}
-norm error in the linear elasticity problem); nevertheless, it is still outperformed by RBNO.
